# PR quality gate

How every pull request to KratosForge gets an engineering quality decision. It sits beside the hardening release gates (`docs/hardening/readiness-checklist.md`, PR #137) and the usability gates (`docs/ux/release-review.md`, PR #166) and doesn't repeat them: those ask "is it safe" and "is it usable"; this asks "does this code deserve to exist in this form". Release management decides merges and promotes; this review recommends, and never merges or deploys.

## Scoring

Each category is scored 0 to 10 from evidence in the diff, the tests and the [standards](architecture.md#standards-the-code-follows).

| 2026-10-29 | #1084 43c2de09 (#996 follow-up, PB-073) | The notebook memory limit counts the processes a notebook's cells start | PASS 9.2 | _memory sums the kernel's pid with its tree: the Job Object pid list on Windows (private bytes per process, so shared pages aren't double counted) and /proc parent links on Linux. When the list can't be read it falls back to the kernel alone. On ERROR_MORE_DATA the list is re-read at the size Windows reports, up to three tries, and a fake-kernel32 test covers it. The stop message says child processes count. Nit: on Linux, VmRSS of forked children includes shared copy-on-write pages, so a fork-based pool can be overcounted; Windows is the product platform. The new subprocess test exceeds the limit through a child process. test_kernel_process and silent_except: 42 passed on 0e789e50 and 43 on 43c2de09. | Category | Weight | A 9 looks like | A 5 looks like |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Functional correctness | 20% | Does what it says on every path, including failures; nothing reports success after a failure | Happy path works; some failure path guesses, or reports success |
| Architectural consistency | 15% | Uses the existing helper for each concern (client factory, `fsutil`, `sqlite_util`, `retrying`, `redact`, `timeutil`, `confirmDialog`, `attempt`) | Adds a new copy of something the repo already has |
| Code quality and maintainability | 15% | Small, named for what it does, no dead code, no broad silent `except` | Long functions, silent handlers, unexplained `type: ignore` |
| UX consistency and simplicity | 15% | Uses shared components, fits the navigation budget, no new configuration | Bespoke dialog or tab bar, a new setting that could be automatic |
| Testing and verification | 15% | A failing-first test for every behavior change, failure paths tested, test names match what they prove | Tests only assert what a fake was told to return |
| Security | 10% | Hardening gates hold; no new scope without `docs/permissions.md` | Unreviewed token, permission or file handling |
| Performance and efficiency | 5% | No new polling or repeated remote calls; local work stays local | New loop or repeated lookup with no limit |
| Documentation | 5% | The doc section exists, matches behavior and says what is local, compatible, simulated, remote or unsupported | Feature names repeated without behavior |

**Decision:** 9.0 and up PASS; 8.0 to 8.9 CONDITIONAL PASS (named follow-up, verified by Release management before the stated point); below 8.0 FAIL.

## Automatic failures

Any one of these is FAIL whatever the score, and can't be conditional:

1. A critical security vulnerability.
2. A known data-loss defect, including an overwrite or delete wider than the user asked for.
3. Functionality presented as working that isn't: an ignored argument, a hard-coded "measured" value, a success message after a failure.
4. A claim of test success that wasn't run.
5. A broken critical workflow.
6. A material architecture change without agreement (a new process, framework, store format or pattern for a concern the repo already handles).
7. Missing authorization control on a write.
8. A known high-severity regression.

## Investigation triggers

These start a closer look; they aren't failures on their own.

| In the diff | Check |
| --- | --- |
| `except Exception` | Does it tell "not set up" from "failed"? Does a failure reach the user? (QA-013) |
| `write_text(json.dumps(...))`, `sqlite3.connect`, `datetime.now`, `re.compile` of secret patterns | Use the shared helper (QA-011, QA-022, QA-024, QA-016) |
| `FabricClient(`, `FileStore(`, `._token(`, `requests.` | Use `Service.fabric` / `files` / `token` (QA-014) |
| A new name in `server.METHODS` | Is it called from the UI or tagged? Is `audit.READ_ONLY` right? (QA-015, QA-017) |
| A new `*_service.py` mixin | Could it be a module called by an existing mixin? |
| `showDialog` with a "…?" title, a `tab` button, a `pill-s` span, `setTimeout` loop | Use the shared UI helper (QA-019) |
| "no capacity", "identical to Fabric", "measured" | Is it verified on this execution path? (QA-008) |
| A test with `monkeypatch.setattr(svc, ...)` or a new `FakeFabric` | Does the test prove the code, or the fake? (QA-018) |
| A Fabric-writing button | Is it under a Preview tag? (QA-020) |

## Report format

One block per PR, pasted into the release record by Release management.

```
PR: #<number> <title>
Quality decision: PASS | CONDITIONAL PASS | FAIL
Score: <overall> (correctness x, architecture x, code x, UX x, tests x, security x, performance x, docs x)
Automatic failures: <none, or which and why>
Findings: <QA-id or new id, severity, file:line, one line each>
AI slop: <duplication, needless complexity, incomplete or simulated behavior, or none>
Required remediation: <what must change, or the condition for CONDITIONAL>
Tests: executed and passed | executed and failed | not executed | not verifiable without Fabric
Release decision: <merge now | merge after X | hold>
```

Every review says which tests were actually run in this review. "Static review only" when none were.

The BQ-013 fix (#817) is merged, so the line is wrong, and on public main it reads as an open security finding. Use neutral wording such as "Working", or "Reviews and fixes merged". The rest checks out against main: the inventory edits match the reviews' findings, product_data is consistent, and zensical --strict is clean. Clean merge with main 2225e281. |
| #829 | Re-check: DuckDB held to the core cap | PASS at 4ca5020e (Windows affinity not checked yet) | 9.0 | The required fix is in: in a capped process, `cpu_cap._duckdb_threads` wraps `duckdb.connect` so it defaults to `threads = cores`, keeps a connection's own `threads`, and sets the default connection (used by `duckdb.sql`). from_env runs first in kernel_host.main, before any engine import. My probe, run again (Linux, cap 2): `duckdb.connect()` and `duckdb.sql` both report 2, where f20888e2 reported 4. The kernel test now asserts 1/1/1, plus DuckDB 1, an explicit 3 kept, and the default connection 1. The docs note PCs past 64 logical processors. Still open before Stable (Chris's PC): SetProcessAffinityMask and children inheriting it on Windows. | #829 | Re-check: cancel stops a capped run's own kernel tree (security P3) | CONDITIONAL at f20888e2 | 8.6 | The new commit is right: Cancel calls `stop()` on the run's own process kernel (ProcessKernel.stop ends the tree), and the notebook's session still gets `interrupt()`; a new test covers it. The required fix from b010573c still stands: a capped process's DuckDB isn't held to the cap's thread count (probe: `threads` 4 with the cap at 2), but the docs say it is. Tests run on the merge with main 2bd73f06: 91 passed, 3 skipped (side_by_side, side_by_side_cores, kernel_process, audit, server lanes, silent_except); ui.test 298 passed; browser sbs-cores 4 passed; py_compile clean. |
| #829 | Side by side: Cores on this PC (Match Fabric by default); a capped run gets its own kernel process with CPU affinity | CONDITIONAL at b010573c (not checked on Windows) | 8.6 | Built from Chris's mockup approval ("this looks good", 23:06Z). Logic checked: `fabric_vcores` returns 2 for a Python notebook, or a `%%configure` vCores value; `cores_for` falls back to all cores for Spark or when the request is at least the PC's count; `side_by_side_cores_set` validates the choice and saves it in the notebook's metadata; the read RPC is in READ_ONLY. A capped run uses a fresh process kernel, sets `FABRIC_DESKTOP_CORES` and the affinity, and closes the kernel in `finally`. Cancel interrupts that kernel. **Required:** DuckDB isn't held to the cap's thread count, but docs/side-by-side.md says "DuckDB, Polars and os.cpu_count() are told the same number". Probe (mine, Linux, 4 CPUs, FABRIC_DESKTOP_CORES=2): after `from_env()`, affinity 2, os.cpu_count 2, Polars 2, but `duckdb.connect()` reports `threads` 4. DuckDB sizes its pool from hardware concurrency, not affinity. On a 24-core PC held to 2, DuckDB would run 24 threads on 2 cores, which slows this PC's side and skews the comparison the feature exists for. Fix: give DuckDB connections in a capped process `threads = cores` (for example a default config set in `from_env`), or correct the docs. Also needed before Stable: a Windows check of SetProcessAffinityMask, including children inheriting it. Note: PCs with more than 64 logical processors use processor groups, so the mask covers only the current group. Note: Match Fabric is the default, so existing Python notebooks' side by side runs now use 2 cores unless changed; the docs say so. Tests run on the merge with main 2bd73f06: side_by_side, side_by_side_cores, kernel_process, audit, server lanes and silent_except 90 passed, 3 skipped; ui.test 298 passed; browser sbs 4 passed; zensical --strict clean; product_data consistent; py_compile clean. |

| #799 8e0e0f3e | X-ray leftover staging: prove it first, then delete (Chris's choice), draft | CONDITIONAL 8.6 | Good: _staging_dataflows needs proof from every Dataflow Gen2 that Fabric lists. Its definition (getDefinition, every part decoded) must not name the lakehouse id or name (word-bounded, any case). It must have a Completed refresh that started at least an hour after the lakehouse's last write. An unreadable definition, unreadable history, no completed refresh, an unknown last write or zero listed dataflows each keep the lakehouse. The server checks again before the delete. A staging name dated more than a day ahead is never offered and doesn't decide the newest. The preview lists each dataflow with a tick or cross. xray_removed is pruned after 90 days. The docs say the paired staging warehouse is left alone and that Remove isn't on MCP or the local API. The test covers names-it, quarterly, never-ran, legacy (unreadable) and none-listed. Required fix: the proof covers only the dataflows that Fabric's items API lists, and the docs themselves say older dataflows aren't listed. The zero-listed guard therefore doesn't protect a mixed workspace: one CI/CD dataflow listed and proved, plus an older Gen2 that isn't listed, passes. A quarterly older dataflow staging in that lakehouse would then break. Either enumerate the workspace's dataflows another way with the scopes sign-in already has (for example Power BI's groups/{id}/dataflows) and keep the lakehouse when any of them isn't in Fabric's list, or show with a source that dataflows the API doesn't list never stage in a timestamped StagingLakehouseForDataflows_ lakehouse, and cite it in xray-fixes.md. Also: it conflicts with main ae174223 in app_settings.py and docs/permissions.md, so merge main. | #799 e01a3e08 | Estate X-ray: leftover Dataflow Gen2 staging lakehouses with Remove (draft) | CONDITIONAL 8.2 | The safeguards are careful and the code does what the brief says. The newest StagingLakehouseForDataflows_<time> in a workspace is never offered (staging_split, checked again live). A lakehouse is removable only after 30 days with no write in Tables or Files: a full paginated DFS listing, and an empty one counts from its name's time. An unreadable listing keeps it. The server re-checks before deleting and refuses a renamed lakehouse. The confirm is confirmDialog danger with typeToConfirm "delete". allow_staging_cleanup is IT-only (not in USER_KEYS) and false in RESTRICTED. The scan no longer walks staging tables or flags them as empty. The delete is audited (not in READ_ONLY) and permissions.md names it. Required before PASS: this is the app's first user-triggered delete of a Fabric-managed item. Microsoft Learn (Data in staging items) says "We strongly advise not to access or modify the data in the staging items directly as it might lead to unexpected behavior", and documents only three removal paths: disable staging (data garbage collected after 30 days), delete the dataflow, or delete the workspace. Fabric community threads show refreshes failing with "Staging lakehouse for dataflow with id ... was not found". The PR itself says Fabric doesn't say which staging lakehouse a dataflow uses, so "the newest is in use" plus 30 quiet days is a guess. A dataflow refreshed quarterly, or one whose staged data Fabric garbage collected, would pass both checks. Either (a) show that the older lakehouses are unreferenced, from the dataflow definitions or item relationships, proven in a tenant run (Chris's yes), or (b) ship the finding list-only with Microsoft's sanctioned steps. Chris decides. Also: it conflicts with main e39281d2 in docs/permissions.md. Nits: settings xray_removed grows without bound; a huge leftover makes Preview a long ordered-lane listing. | #793 32bc953f | Entra ID group guidance per domain (Preview) | FAIL 7.4 (security) | Suggested group names per domain role, linking existing groups by object ID, and generated scripts (Graph PowerShell, az bash, CSV, Fabric roles PowerShell); app reads Graph with optional Group.Read.All (docs/permissions.md updated). Bash quoting (_sh), CSV formula guard, Graph filter quoting and _SAFE group names are sound. Blocking security defect: _ps() doubles only ASCII ', but PowerShell's tokenizer treats U+2018, U+2019, U+201A and U+201B as single quotes too (CharTraits.cs IsSingleQuote). Values a domain owner controls reach _ps() in the scripts: a linked group's group_name (link_entra_group strips control characters only), workspace names from product ports, and the domain name. Probed on the PR's tree: group_name x’; Start-Process calc; ’ is emitted as (Get-GroupId 'x’; Start-Process calc; ’' ...) and Write-Host 'x’; ...', so a domain owner's text leaves the string literal and runs as the Fabric or Entra admin who runs the script (a balanced payload such as x’ + (Start-Process calc) + ’ parses cleanly). A plain name like Chris’ HR also breaks the generated script. Fix: in _ps double every one of ' ‘ ’ ‚ ‛ (or replace the curly ones), add tests with each; consider limiting group_name to the characters a script needs. Also: conflicts with main 21d318fc in styles.css and service.py (routine). | #723 2854f6cd | WH-10a re-check: refuses dynamic SQL, checks procedure and function bodies, adds role/user/EXECUTE AS words | CONDITIONAL 8.5 | Merges cleanly; warehouse_queries + sql_exec + silent_except pass; compiles. My earlier cases are now refused (EXEC('COMMIT'), EXEC('GRANT'), sp_executesql, proc body then EXEC, ALTER ROLE, CREATE USER, EXECUTE AS), as are EXEC (@s), EXEC/**/(...), INSERT ... EXEC ('...') and COMMIT WORK. Still allowed by verdicts(writes=True): EXEC [sp_executesql] N'COMMIT', EXEC [sys].[sp_executesql] N'GRANT ...', EXEC "sp_executesql" ..., EXEC [sp_addrolemember] (bracket and quote contents are stripped before the name checks); DECLARE @p sysname = N'sp_executesql'; EXEC @r = @p N'COMMIT' (module name in a variable: the lookahead skips `@r =`); GRANT or USE on the same line as the statement before with no `;` (UPDATE ... WHERE id=1 GRANT ...), after a CR, after a label, or after `IF (1=1)`: these fall to "couldn't be read in full: it runs as written". Fix: refuse a batch sqlglot can't read unless it's a procedure/function definition; match GRANT/REVOKE/DENY/USE (reserved words) anywhere outside strings, comments and quoted names; check procedure names with brackets and quotes unwrapped; refuse EXEC of a module named by a variable (EXEC @r = @p). CREATE SCHEMA ... AUTHORIZATION also passes (ownership). |

| #710 c7f0ccca | kratosforge:// deep links (VS Code Phase 1b, Chris "Go") | FAIL 7.6 (security) | Blocking: a link's notebook path reaches the file system before any check that it's in a known folder. parse() accepts UNC paths (probe: path=%5C%5Cattacker.example%5Cshare%5Cx.ipynb, //attacker.example/share/x.ipynb and \\\\attacker@SSL\\dav\\x.ipynb all Ok(NotebookPath)); showLink sends it to notebook_name_for_path, which calls Path(path).resolve() and is_file() first (probe recorded both calls on //attacker.example/share/x.ipynb). On Windows that opens an SMB (or WebDAV over HTTPS) connection to the named host and sends the user's NTLM credentials, from one click on a web page's link. Fix: refuse anything but a local drive path (X:\ or X:/; no \\, //, \\?\, \\.\, @SSL) in parse() and again in notebook_name_for_path, and check containment lexically (normpath/abspath against each folder) before any resolve, is_file or stat. | #708 7c48bb08 | Prove cancel-while-starting race: Kernel.interrupt armed flag | PASS 9.1 (Windows CI pending) | Locking is sound: interrupt sets cancel_requested and reads armed under run.cancel_lock; _execute sets armed and reads cancel_requested under the same lock, so either interrupt sees armed and raises in the thread, or the thread sees the request and raises itself inside the outer try (one "Cancelled" message, status cancelled, writes None, watches skipped). No cancel can be lost between arming and running; an async exception landing just after the lock is released is still inside the outer try. New test fails on main, passes on head; prove interrupt/cancel tests 4/4 five times; debugger, debugging, kernel_process, magics, parity, pipeline_notebooks, prove, shared_keys, shared_sessions + silent_except 263 passed; py311 compile; merges cleanly. Merge only once sidecar (windows-latest) is green. Nit: armed is never cleared, so an interrupt that lands after the handlers (in the finally, before run.finish) can still end the thread without finishing the run; set armed = False under the lock at the start of the finally. |

No token or connection secret is stored, but the error text is not redacted and can echo row values (a conversion error quotes the value). Defect: the caps are per warehouse with no total budget. In Chromium one warehouse at the cap (about 4M chars) fits and a second throws QuotaExceededError (my probe). sqlHistoryAdd swallows that, so history stops recording for good, and the shared origin quota is left too small for saveQueryTabs, which then fails and drops unsaved tabs on restart. Fix: a total budget across warehouses (about 1 MB), drop the oldest and retry on QuotaExceededError, and keep only the error kind or a short redacted error. Keep the caller's cells as a version on changed_on_disk too, so both sides survive. (3) If a cell is running, loadIntoState keeps the running state: Load leaves diskConflict true with a stale stamp, so autosave stays off, and the 'Loaded' toast is wrong. | #557 2e63fba8 | Pop-out windows (Preview): Monitor's From KratosForge and Fabric Monitoring hub in windows of their own; new feature, verdict only (needs Chris's go) | PASS 9.0 | Correctness 9.0, architecture 9.0, code 9.0, UX 9.1, tests 8.9, security 9.0, performance 9.0, docs 9.1. Merges clean on main f7360a79; on the merged tree ui.test 260/260, browser pop-out 9/9, csp 11/11, back-forward 4/4. cargo tests not run here (the crate builds only on Windows; owner reports 60). Security: (1) A pop-out is the app's own page (WebviewUrl::App), so it gets the configured CSP. on_navigation(app_page) keeps it on tauri://localhost / http(s)://tauri.localhost (no user, no port) and sends web links to the browser. The pop-out Fabric view is built by the same fabric_view_show code: fabric_nav_allows, the same pop-up filter with the #455 hosts, the SSO environment and the saved cookies, so it shares the sign-in profile; Sign out closes it. (2) The capability names only the app-page webviews pop-out-runs/pop-out-fabric, not the docked "fabric-pop-out" Fabric view, which is stricter than main's window-wide match. Without an app ACL manifest, the app pages can call the same app commands as main; that is the same code and origin, not a widening. (3) fabric_view_show/hide/action resolve their view from the calling window through fabric_view_label, which accepts only "main" and "pop-out-fabric". A call from the Fabric page would resolve to the same view as before, so the embedded page gains nothing new; pop_out_open_in_fabric passes only pages fabric_page accepts. (4) QA-008: the docs make no capacity claims. Nit: rpc starts the sidecar on demand, and only update_app sets `updating`. If the main page re-ran checkComponents (a reload) while the Runs pop-out refreshes every 15 s, a refresh could start the old engine during update_sidecar's install. Startup can't overlap today, since pop-outs close with main. |
| #556 665b0492 | Re-check: semantic model table-name matcher and plan cache | PASS 9.1 | Correctness 9.1, architecture 9.0, code 9.0, UX 9.0, tests 9.1, security 9.5, performance 9.2, docs 9.0. Merges clean on main f7360a79. test_cloud + test_meta + silent_except 73/73; py3.11 compile ok. Matcher probes: a "///" description before the table line, a property before lineageTag, a measure's twice-indented tag before the table's own, and CRLF with a quoted '' name all match; a file with only a column's tag gives nothing. Cache: only meta_model_plan reads with fresh=False. meta_build_model with replace always reads fresh, and stores that read, before plan, build and save. The plan's answer is display only, never passed to the build, so a stale cache can at most make the preview differ from what Update then writes; it can't steer the build. A failed fresh read still stops before writing (test at line 420). |
| #556 f54d9fbd | Semantic model follow-up (fix): Update reads the model's TMDL (getDefinition), maps tables to entities by lineage tag and keeps their names; only a clashing newcomer is schema-qualified; an unreadable definition stops before writing | PASS 9.0 | Correctness 8.9, architecture 9.1, code 9.0, UX 9.0, tests 9.0, security 9.5, performance 8.8, docs 9.0. Merges clean on main d6099321. test_cloud + test_meta + silent_except 73/73; 3 new test_cloud tests fail on main's code; py3.11 compile ok. Kept names are reserved up front (taken), so an entity sorted earlier can't take one, and a kept name used twice is dropped. Without replace nothing is read; a getDefinition failure raises before plan, build or save. table_names probe: plain and quoted names and CRLF match. Recommended follow-up (not blocking): the regex needs "table X" as the file's first line, immediately followed by lineageTag. A table someone gave a description in Fabric (TMDL writes it as a "///" line before "table"), or a property before lineageTag, isn't matched (probe: {}). That table then gets default naming, so in the clash case it could still be renamed. Searching for the "table" line and the first table-level lineageTag anywhere in the file would cover it. Merges clean on main 79eb8d4b. engine_patch.rs, built standalone on Linux (serde_json, offline): its 6 unit tests pass. Integrity holds. version.json goes through update_guard::check_manifest (stamp ordering) before the zip is fetched; the zip is checked against engine_update_sha256; check_patched requires the release's commit and runtime, python.exe and fabric_desktop/__init__.py before put_engine_in_place; the stamp is remembered only after that. Any Err removes .new and falls back to the full download, so no partial engine is left in place. CI refuses a build without a 64-hex runtime; the update zip is attested and listed in the per-build release; promote.yml verifies engine_update_sha256 when present and deletes a stale update zip from stable when the promoted build has none. Blocking: with may_move, stage_runtime renames the source engine into .new and strips it. If the unpack or check then fails, install_engine deletes .new, so that engine is gone, and only the full download can replace it. Probe (standalone test): stage_runtime(src, .new, true) then the error path's remove_dir_all(.new) leaves src missing. update_sidecar stops the sidecar first, so the move normally succeeds. After an exe update, engine\<BUILD_COMMIT> doesn't exist yet and the previous build's engine is the one engine_python() falls back to, so a failed update plus a failed full download (e.g. offline) leaves the PC with no engine, where main keeps the old one until a new engine is in place. "is about to be removed anyway" holds only on success, and docs/security.md says the zip is applied "only to a copy". To re-pass: move only when engine\<BUILD_COMMIT> exists (else copy), or restore the source on failure; add a test; align the security.md wording. |

| #510 0a2539a7 | Magics: NOT NULL check raises PySpark's FIELD_NOT_NULLABLE_WITH_NAME; verifySchema=False skips it; structs in arrays and maps checked | PASS | 9.2 | Closes the three #497 nits. (1) A null in a NOT NULL field raises PySparkValueError (a ValueError) with "[FIELD_NOT_NULLABLE_WITH_NAME] field id: This field is not nullable, but got None.", PySpark 3.4+'s text. (2) createDataFrame takes samplingRatio and verifySchema as PySpark does. verifySchema=False skips the check, and neither is passed on to DuckDB, which refused both with NotImplementedError. (3) Structs inside array elements and map values are walked ("field a.element.x", "field m.value.x"). Reviewer's probe confirmed all of these, and confirmed that verifySchema=False and samplingRatio=0.5 now create the frame. Cost: the early return for all-nullable schemas is gone, so every StructType schema walks the rows. | #495 fd54b0c5 | Runtime (stacked on #490): a notebook process's output files go when it stops, restarts or closes | PASS | 9.2 | Closes the #490 kernel_process gap. Each ProcessKernel makes its own folder (mkdtemp, kratosforge-kernel-*, 0700) and passes it to the child as FABRIC_DESKTOP_OUTPUT_DIR; notebook.py makes its spill there with mkstemp. The folder is emptied after Stop and before every new process (restart, crash, limit kill), close() removes it, and sweep_spills also covers files in, and empty folders of, kratosforge-kernel-* older than 1 h. Race check: the pre-start clear runs under the kernel's lock and only when the process is gone (poll() not None); Stop clears only after kill and proc.wait(10). A dead process holds no handle, and only the kernel process itself writes spills (cell subprocesses don't), so there is no live writer to race. If wait(10) times out, Stop raises before clearing and the files wait for the next start or sweep: safe. Env var: the parent sets it from its own mkdtemp path. The parent only ever clears its stored output_dir, and only kratosforge-output-*.txt in it. User code that changes the variable can only make a new random-named file (mkstemp never overwrites), which needs no more than the code execution it already has. Nit: the sweep can rmdir the empty folder of a second engine's kernel that has been idle for an hour; that kernel's next spill then fails to create its file and says "The rest wasn't kept.", with no crash. The 2 new tests fail with #490's code and pass here; kernel_process, output_cap, kernel_sessions and silent_except 61 passed (pinned venv). Merges cleanly on main. Merge order: #490, #495. |

(2) Errors bypass the 16 MB rich cap: 15 html outputs, the truncation note, then the ValueError. (3) "con .txt" and "prn  .x" are refused. Sweep safety: on Windows the Temp folder is per-user; on Linux /tmp is sticky, so another user's 0600 file can't be unlinked (the OSError is suppressed). The worst case is that an output line names a file from a finished cell over an hour old that is gone. Fix: build these frames without named views (relation.filter for dropna/na.drop, aggregate and union for describe), or tie each view's lifetime to the result and everything derived from it; add a loop test asserting the view count stays bounded. Only SQL UNPIVOT drops them by default (AstBuilder adds Filter(IsNotNull) unless INCLUDE NULLS). Ours drops them (the probe's (1,'q',NULL) row is missing), and the test_spark_api.py comment at line 70 is wrong. The INCLUDE NULLS fix and the test correction can ride the fix push. So transforms that aren't inverses no longer recurse: (v*2, v*3) and (v+1, identity) settle after one update each way, out of step but with no error, as in traitlets. Only an observer that changes the source mid-update raises Broken link. The coordinator's summary ("non-inverse transforms now raise") overstates it, but the docs sentence is accurate. TraitError is exported on the ipywidgets module. Reviewer's probe: unlink stops syncing; dlink copies one way only. A sibling with a shared prefix (Files/raw to Files/raw2) is allowed, and dbutils.fs inherits the check. Reviewer's probe /mnt/project-files/quality/repros/r477_self_move_probe.py. Finding (critical, data loss, pre-existing but inside this PR's fix): local paths are compared with os.path.abspath, which doesn't resolve links. fs.mv("file:.../data/a.txt", "file:.../alias.txt", overwrite=True), where alias.txt is a symlink to a.txt (or a path through a Windows junction), is allowed: cp writes the same bytes through the link, the size check passes, and rm(src) deletes the only copy (re-run by the reviewer on this head: symlink_file case, source gone). So those cells lose their progress line. Sampling the cell thread during the %%sql query found it 26 of 26 times in notebook:as_table <- display <- _show <- _run_sql. | #468 9dee705 | One sign-in client at a time; get_status doesn't build one when nobody signed in (conformance flake cause) | PASS | 9.2 | (1) No deadlock: _auth_lock (a plain Lock) is held only while the Authenticator is built and is released before it is returned; every sign-in (interactive WAM, browser, silent token) runs on the returned object outside the lock, and Authenticator.__init__ never calls back into the service. A slow or stalled endpoint lookup now makes the other callers wait for the one build rather than each doing their own, and they all needed the client anyway. (2) Skipping can't hide a sign-in: get_accounts() reads MSAL's token cache, broker sign-ins included, and _save writes that cache to msal_cache.bin (default_cache_path, the same path the service's Authenticator uses) after every change; WAM accounts the app never signed in with aren't listed by get_accounts on main either. So with no client in this process and no cache file, main also answered not signed in. (3) Right after a sign-in in this engine, _auth exists, so get_status reads it; a sign-in by another process (a scheduled run) writes the cache file, which the next get_status sees. A changed client ID still rebuilds the client, now under the lock. Both new tests fail with main's two files (reviewer ran them) and pass here; integrations_conformance 39/39 three times in a row; integrations_core, auth and silent_except 65 passed. No assertion or time limit changed. Merges cleanly on main. |

Finding (medium, memory): Out keeps the last OUT_KEEP = 100 results and each one stays alive (reviewer's probe /mnt/project-files/quality/repros/r467_history_probe.py: 30 shown results of 8 MB each, all 30 still alive). | #465 14cbc50 | AI tool Python keeps the Windows processor variables (fixes a #249 regression) | PASS | 9.2 | Cause confirmed in CPython 3.13: platform._get_machine_win32 falls back to PROCESSOR_ARCHITEW6432 then PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE when its WMI query raises OSError, and _Processor.get_win32 falls back to PROCESSOR_IDENTIFIER; with #249's allowlist both came back empty, and Polars' CPU check then refuses to import. Security: the four values are hardware descriptors Windows sets (architecture names like AMD64, a CPU family string, a count). They hold no secret and no path, and any process can read the same facts through WMI or GetSystemInfo, so passing them gives an approved tool nothing new; the allowlist still drops every other variable (test_the_external_environment_is_the_allowlist_and_nothing_else passes). Minimal: the architecture pair and PROCESSOR_IDENTIFIER are what CPython reads; NUMBER_OF_PROCESSORS is not read by CPython or any package in the pinned engine (os.cpu_count uses the system call), so it could go, though it is harmless. The new test forces the WMI failure and expects AMD64; it fails with main's kernel_proc.py (reviewer ran it) and passes here. docs/permissions.md lists the four. integrations_executions and silent_except 77 passed. Merges cleanly on main. Nit: three blank lines before the new test and one after it. |

| #258 3a05d36 | HD-022 update hand-over, re-check after merging main (#264's Arc<Sidecar>) | PASS | 9.1 | Checked the main.rs resolution with --remerge-diff: rpc is main's (lock held only to find or start the engine, ptr_eq before clearing the slot) plus the updating check under the engine lock; start_into fills Option<Arc<Sidecar>>; shut_down is reworked for the Arc. (1) Killing with holders: shut_down takes the engine out of the slot first, so no call can clone it afterwards and strong_count counts only calls in flight. A killed engine can't hand a half reply to a call: read_replies delivers a line only when reply_id parses it as whole JSON, so a truncated last line is dropped, and EOF closes Pending, so every waiting call gets Closed (SIDECAR_EXITED) and drops its Arc; a call writing to stdin gets a broken pipe and returns the same. The kill and wait paths lock child with unwrap_or_else(into_inner), and nothing panics while holding the sidecar, child, stdin or pending locks, so no mutex is poisoned. (2) Err path: hand_over calls resume(false) when stop_engine fails, which clears updating and shows the window; nothing was swapped. The slot is already empty and that engine's process was killed (an Err needs a holder, and holders trigger the kill), so the next call starts a fresh engine and the old exe keeps working. (3) Deadlocks: the lock order is sidecar then child in rpc, start_into and resume; shut_down takes each one alone and holds neither while it waits; rpc's ended branch takes only sidecar; stop_kernel takes only child; updating is an atomic, not a lock. The 10 s wait and the 90 s start wait block one async worker; rpc calls finish on spawn_blocking threads, so holders can still return. A grandchild holding the engine's stdout open would delay EOF past the grace period; that fails safe (Err, no swap), and the engine's response handle is not inherited. Rust can't be compiled here (no WebKitGTK): the CI shell job (cargo test, clippy) must be green on this head before merge, and the owner reports cargo test 56 passed. Windows file locks during the swap stay with Chris's Windows check. Merges cleanly on main. |

| #463 9b80da6 | debug_cell never starts a kernel session (follow-up to #264) | PASS | 9.3 | debug_cell looks the session up with create=False and answers "No cell is running under the debugger." when there is none, so a quick-lane debugger command no longer makes a session (and, at the session cap, no longer evicts another notebook's idle session). On main it made a session but no process (debug sends with spawn=False). The new test fails on main (reviewer ran it with main's service.py: 1 failed) and passes on this head. debugger, kernel_sessions, server_lanes and silent_except 63 passed. Merges cleanly on main. On the other quick-lane calls the PR names: KernelSessions.session holds an RLock, so a quick-lane create can't race the ordered lane into two sessions. poll_cell, interrupt_cell and kernel_variables never start a process (they answer idle, not interrupted or [] when none runs), but a lookup that creates a session calls _make_room, which at the cap closes the oldest idle session and clears its variables. notebook.js calls kernel_variables when a notebook opens, so opening or refreshing a notebook with no session can evict another notebook's idle session. That predates #264 (it happened in the ordered lane too) and is worth a small follow-up: create=False in those three, returning their existing empty answers. kernel_complete starts a process by design (completion needs one), so it keeps create=True. |

| #441 1cb7388 (re-grade) | Idle sweep never makes a second session | PASS | 9.2 | Reworked as suggested: reap_idle moves stale sessions into _reaping under the lock; session() takes a reaping session back (touched) instead of making a new one; forget() checks _reaping; reap_idle closes only a session still in _reaping and not running, and puts a running one back. Reviewer's probe (/mnt/project-files/quality/repros/r441b_reaping_probe.py): a lookup during the sweep gets the same kernel, nothing is closed and nothing is left untracked, whether or not a cell starts; an idle session still closes and the next lookup makes a fresh one. The earlier r441 probe's two cases no longer happen. Nit: switch() clears _sessions but not _reaping, so after a quick process to in-engine to process switch a lookup could take back a pre-switch kernel; the sweep closes it otherwise. A caller that already held the kernel (no new lookup) and starts a cell between the running check and close is the same window as main. kernel_sessions, kernel_process, prove, silent_except tests plus the r441b and r379 probes 103 passed. Merges cleanly with main. |
| #441 ee27ff8 | Idle sweep closes a second session instead of dropping it | CONDITIONAL | 8.6 | Fixes the #435 nit for the common case: the old session with the running cell goes back in, and no process is left untracked by the sweep. Two gaps in the race it targets, both confirmed by the reviewer's probe (/mnt/project-files/quality/repros/r441_reap_second_session_probe.py). (1) The new session was handed to a caller by session()/kernel() (that lookup is what made it), and closing it strands that caller: its kernel is closed and out of the list, and ProcessKernel._ensure starts a fresh process on its next cell, which runs untracked, with variables apart from the notebook's session, and is never reaped, listed or closed by forget. (2) When both sessions have a running cell, the old one is closed mid-cell, which is worse than main, where it was only left untracked. Suggested fix: never make the second session. reap_idle keeps the sessions it takes out in a _reaping map under the lock, and session() moves a reaping session back (touched) instead of creating a new one; reap_idle then closes only what is still out and not running. kernel_sessions, kernel_process, prove, silent_except tests plus the probe 99 passed. Merges cleanly with main 535dea3. |

| #326 | OneLake write lease: docs, holder host, TTL 60 s / wait 90 s | PASS holds at 247ba2c | 9.2 | Docs now agree (agent.md Limits, notebooks.md write_table row naming the lease file and the 90 s wait, teams.md). LeaseBusy names owner and host. **TTL probe:** a write is renewed while it runs. `Held` replaces the lease every ttl/3 (20 s) on its own thread for the whole block. Probe repros/r326b_long_write.py, with TTL 0.6 s and a write lasting 3 s (5x TTL): an intruder trying every 0.2 s never got the lease, and the write committed. With every renewal failing past the TTL, the write ends with LeaseLost (reported, not silent). Metadata runs still pass ttl=seconds (120 s or `table_lease_seconds`), so only notebook and job writes use 60 s. Residual margins, worth a follow-up: each OneLake renew is up to 3 requests with `timeout=60`, so one hung renew can outlast a 60 s TTL and let another writer take over before it is marked lost (it is then reported as LeaseLost after the write); a renew timeout of ttl/3 or less would close that. Expiry compares the writer's clock against the reader's, so tolerance for clock skew between PCs drops to about 40 s. Tests run: probe 2 + test_table_leases, test_leases_local, test_agent 39 passed. Clean merge with main. |

Probes (repros/r326_lease_probe.py, 6 passed): a failing write and a KeyboardInterrupt mid-write both release the lease; a stale lease is taken over and the write goes ahead; an error from the lease store stops the write with the table unchanged; with an old agent holding the LH_Meta lease the table lease is never taken; an exception inside a run releases both. In Engine.run a LeaseBusy lands in the run's own except, so the run fails and nothing is written. The lease file is the only new write into user lakehouses. Nits, worth fixing in this PR: docs/agent.md:366 still says 'The desktop app and runs in Fabric don't take write leases', which now contradicts line 23; notebooks.md's write_table row doesn't say it can wait up to a minute or that it writes a lease file in the lakehouse's Files (only agent.md names it); teams.md:67 still says two PCs' runs can overwrite each other. Behaviour notes: after a kernel is killed mid-write, the 120 s lease outlives the 60 s notebook wait, so a write in the next two minutes fails 'being written by KratosForge'; every desktop has owner 'KratosForge', so the message doesn't say which PC (the body has host, `holder()` doesn't show it). | #322 | HD-037: Stop ends every process below the kernel, including ones in their own session or group | PASS at c8fc178 | 9.2 | `_signal_tree` SIGSTOPs the kernel, then every descendant found by ppid (/proc, or ps on macOS), rescanning up to 10 rounds until nothing new appears; then SIGKILLs each one and the group. Probes. Fork during the scan: a stopped process can't fork, and each round stops what it finds. PID reuse: a stopped process can't exit and its stopped parent can't reap it, so found pids stay theirs until the kill. The residual window is a pid that exits between being listed and being stopped, which is negligible. Only processes with a ppid chain to the kernel are signalled. The root is signalled only when `stop()` or `_end` saw `proc.poll() is None` under the lock, as before. Not covered (as before): a process that daemonized by double fork before Stop (reparented to init), and children of a kernel that had already crashed. Evidence: test_kernel_process 15 passed in 3 of 3 runs; with main's kernel_proc.py, 2 failed and left exactly 4 `sleep(120)` processes running (3 session leaders + 1 group member), which I killed; the head's runs left none. Tests run: validate + prove + notebook tests 170 passed. Clean merge with main. |

| #316 | SCD1 and SCD2 wizard templates; `table_exists` in Fabric's lakehouse module | PASS at 6b924ce (draft; writes only the dimension the user names, as an overwrite the template states) | 9.2 | SCD2 logic, probed through a real Delta round trip (`quality/repros/r316_scd2_edges.py`). Unchanged rows stay current and keep their `valid_from`. A changed tracked column closes the row (`valid_to` = the new version's `valid_from`, the same run time) and adds version+1. New keys get version 1. A key that leaves the source stays current; if it comes back changed, it gets version 2. A NaN in a tracked float column doesn't make a new version on every run. One current row per key holds, with Int64 version and Datetime(us, UTC) types after the round trip. Null and duplicate source keys are stopped by the check cell. Late-arriving rows with their own effective dates aren't supported: validity is the run's UTC time, as the docs say, and the proof contract (keys + `version`, ignoring `valid_from` and `valid_to`) matches that. The Fabric shim's `table_exists` treats only not-found as no, so any other error is raised, never turned into an overwrite. Nit (docs, not blocking): with tracked columns named, a change to an untracked column alone is not written at all; the current row keeps the old value (phone 1 → 999 stays 1). Say so in the template text and in notebooks.md, or update untracked columns in place. | #281 | Test fix: the paused-agent takeover test waits for etl-01's step before freezing it | PASS at cd6174e (test-only) | 9.3 | Root cause holds, and the product is right under that ordering. `Agent.step` (agent.py:333-353) submits the job to the pool and then calls `publish_status`, which rewrites `agents/etl-01.json`. The old test's job thread marked etl-01 lost and polled etl-02; when the publish landed between those two (slow runner), etl-01 looked live, so etl-02 rightly didn't take over and etl-01 ran it: an agent that is still publishing status *is* live, and `_guarded`'s `taken_over` check (agent.py:361-364) only cancels when another agent actually took the request. Reproduced the exact Windows failure (`'succeeded' == 'cancelled'`) on main's test by delaying `publish_status` 0.3 s and waiting 0.6 s between `lost` and `poll`; the same probes pass on #281's test. The new test runs `step()` then sets an event the job waits on, so `lost` always follows the publish; assertions unchanged, nothing skipped. Run: the test 5/5 in a row; test_agent_takeover.py 16/16. |

| #270 | Kernel: a poll or interrupt never starts a kernel process (fixes red main on Windows) | PASS at 4b0c632 | 9.3 | Root cause confirmed as a real product race, not a flaky test: when the limits watcher ends the process, a poll that checked `proc.poll()` just before it died reached `_request`, whose `_ensure` spawned a fresh process that answered "idle". That explains both Windows failures on main fc3bd71: the memory test got `idle`, and the interrupt test got `_limit_note`'s "Variables from earlier cells are kept" on that idle answer (execution_count 0, seconds 0). Fix: `_request(spawn=False)` for poll and both interrupts; a dead or missing process raises KernelGone, which poll already turns into `_stuck` or the ended cell; a cell start still spawns. The new test fails on main's kernel_proc.py with the same `'idle' == 'error'` and passes with the fix. Run: test_kernel_process.py 13/13 three runs in a row; full sidecar 2103 passed, 3 skipped. Main 7d3b82c's own Windows run passed (the race is intermittent), so #270's Windows CI is the confirmation. #268 closed. |

Linux verified: `test_stop_ends_the_processes_a_cell_started_too` and the kernel suites 12/12. Code: POSIX `start_new_session` + `killpg(SIGKILL)`; Windows job with KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, typed ctypes handles, falls back to killing the kernel only if the job can't be made; every former `proc.kill()` (stop, limits, failed start) now ends the tree. Nit: on Linux/macOS a kernel that crashes leaves the processes its cells started running, because `release()` ("processes a crashed kernel left behind") only acts on Windows; `killpg` on the old group would cover it |
